The per-household section, and everything this change builds on it, rests on a solver-arm statistic that a code bug biases. runPolicy (src/solver/solve.js:967; the same line in the K5 snapshot 31a1b52 at l.964) returns spendYears, atTarget and aboveTarget on a failed path, but not belowSum or aboveSum. statsFlex (experiment.mjs:161-162) reads the missing fields as 0 ('// 0'), so every below-target and above-target year on a failed path counts at level 0. The guardrails arm's runFixedPath (experiment.mjs:136) returns both fields, so the two arms' statistic differs in fact (fair-test variable 29), and the gate could not see it. Proof from the solver's own per-path records (results/k5-*/S*.solver.record.json.gz): the JSON's levelWhenBelowMean is reproduced to four decimals only when belowSum is 0 on failed paths. It cuts more at 16 of 24 points (0.64-3.56), not all 24 (3.02-5.49). S330 cuts more at 0 of 24, not 20; S184 at 8, not 20; S354 at 0, not 3. (c) Dead years count as no cut (level 0), so an arm that runs out more often shows fewer cut years, a bias toward the trade reading. On S126 the compiled moves carry cash at sigma 0.0157 and V 0 (the library preset: Cash Equivalents volatility 0.00, sigmaParam 1.57; engine l.2475 and realAt alike).
